It hasn't really caused any issues with them.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific happened related to your freelance business that started making you feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. I started getting this overwhelmed—and, you know, avoidant about everything—about a month after I took on this community arts contract. I was trying to make the business more formal at the time, and I guess that's when it all just started feeling like too much to handle.\n\nDoctor: Did the overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start immediately after taking on the community arts contract or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned just a second ago, there was a little bit of a delay. It was about a month after I took on that contract and started trying to make the business more formal. So it wasn't right away... it just took a few weeks for it all to really hit me.\n\nDoctor: Did your overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start after some significant change or stressor in your life, like taking on the community arts contract?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was just saying, that was definitely the main thing. I started getting really overwhelmed and avoidant about a month after I took on that community arts contract and tried to make the business more formal. That was the big change that set all this off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant emotional or behavioral symptoms, such as depression, in addition to the anxiety and avoidance related to your freelance business?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I've been feeling depressed or anything like that. It's really just the stress and the anxiety about the work, and wanting to avoid it. But I don't feel sad or down in the dumps.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance of freelance tasks would improve if the stressor, such as the community arts contract, were removed or resolved?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would, yeah. But, um, the contract and all those business changes are still happening right now. So I haven't really had a stretch of time where the stressor or its fallout has been over for months to see if the anxiety actually goes away. It's all just still ongoing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I end up crying at my kitchen table, or I'm forgetting normal stuff, like paying my own bills. It's just getting to be too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where you suddenly felt intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that. I mean, I get really stressed out and overwhelmed, but it's not like a sudden panic or intense fear that hits me all at once. It's more of a constant, heavy feeling of stress that just builds up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or tasks because of feelings of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly just what I mentioned earlier. I definitely avoid the freelance stuff—like opening client emails or looking at the invoices. I just put them off because dealing with them makes me too anxious. But outside of that... no, I'm not really avoiding other situations or anything. It's really just those specific business tasks.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety mainly about your freelance business or does it also affect other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It's really just about the freelance business. I mean, like I said, it kind of spills over when I end up forgetting to pay my own personal bills because I'm so stressed out. But the actual anxiety itself... it's just tied to the business and dealing with the clients. The rest of my life is pretty normal, honestly. I don't really feel anxious about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My relationships are fine. I mean, I might be a little distracted sometimes if I'm stressing over an email I'm avoiding, but... no, I still see my friends and talk to my family just fine. It hasn't really caused any issues with them.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific happened related to your freelance business that started making you feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. I started getting this overwhelmed—and, you know, avoidant about everything—about a month after I took on this community arts contract. I was trying to make the business more formal at the time, and I guess that's when it all just started feeling like too much to handle.\n\nDoctor: Did the overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start immediately after taking on the community arts contract or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned just a second ago, there was a little bit of a delay. It was about a month after I took on that contract and started trying to make the business more formal. So it wasn't right away... it just took a few weeks for it all to really hit me.\n\nDoctor: Did your overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start after some significant change or stressor in your life, like taking on the community arts contract?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was just saying, that was definitely the main thing. I started getting really overwhelmed and avoidant about a month after I took on that community arts contract and tried to make the business more formal. That was the big change that set all this off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant emotional or behavioral symptoms, such as depression, in addition to the anxiety and avoidance related to your freelance business?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I've been feeling depressed or anything like that. It's really just the stress and the anxiety about the work, and wanting to avoid it. But I don't feel sad or down in the dumps.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance of freelance tasks would improve if the stressor, such as the community arts contract, were removed or resolved?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would, yeah. But, um, the contract and all those business changes are still happening right now. So I haven't really had a stretch of time where the stressor or its fallout has been over for months to see if the anxiety actually goes away. It's all just still ongoing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would go away if you were able to find a way to successfully manage the stress of your freelance business?\n\nPatient: I mean, I'm not really sure. I haven't figured out how to manage it yet, so it's hard to say if the anxiety would actually go away. I'd like to think it would, but I just don't know right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance of freelance tasks are a direct reaction to the stress of taking on the community arts contract and trying to formalize your business?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That's actually exactly why I came in. Ever since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I just... I keep getting so wound up over invoices and client messages. And then I avoid them until I'm sitting there crying at my kitchen table, or I end up forgetting normal stuff like paying my own bills. So, yeah, it's definitely a direct reaction to all that new stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would disappear if the stress related to your freelance business were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would, yeah. But like I was saying a minute ago, the contract and all those changes with the business are still happening right now. So... I just haven't had a stretch of time where the stress or the fallout from it has been over for months to actually see if the anxiety completely disappears. But the actual anxiety itself... it's just tied to the business and dealing with the clients. The rest of my life is pretty normal, honestly. I don't really feel anxious about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My relationships are fine. I mean, I might be a little distracted sometimes if I'm stressing over an email I'm avoiding, but... no, I still see my friends and talk to my family just fine. It hasn't really caused any issues with them.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific happened related to your freelance business that started making you feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. I started getting this overwhelmed—and, you know, avoidant about everything—about a month after I took on this community arts contract. I was trying to make the business more formal at the time, and I guess that's when it all just started feeling like too much to handle.\n\nDoctor: Did the overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start immediately after taking on the community arts contract or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned just a second ago, there was a little bit of a delay. It was about a month after I took on that contract and started trying to make the business more formal. So it wasn't right away... it just took a few weeks for it all to really hit me.\n\nDoctor: Did your overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start after some significant change or stressor in your life, like taking on the community arts contract?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was just saying, that was definitely the main thing. I started getting really overwhelmed and avoidant about a month after I took on that community arts contract and tried to make the business more formal. That was the big change that set all this off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant emotional or behavioral symptoms, such as depression, in addition to the anxiety and avoidance related to your freelance business?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I've been feeling depressed or anything like that. It's really just the stress and the anxiety about the work, and wanting to avoid it. But I don't feel sad or down in the dumps.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance of freelance tasks would improve if the stressor, such as the community arts contract, were removed or resolved?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would, yeah. But, um, the contract and all those business changes are still happening right now. So I haven't really had a stretch of time where the stressor or its fallout has been over for months to see if the anxiety actually goes away. It's all just still ongoing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would go away if you were able to find a way to successfully manage the stress of your freelance business?\n\nPatient: I mean, I'm not really sure. I haven't figured out how to manage it yet, so it's hard to say if the anxiety would actually go away. I'd like to think it would, but I just don't know right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance of freelance tasks are a direct reaction to the stress of taking on the community arts contract and trying to formalize your business?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That's actually exactly why I came in. Ever since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I just... I keep getting so wound up over invoices and client messages. And then I avoid them until I'm sitting there crying at my kitchen table, or I end up forgetting normal stuff like paying my own bills. So, yeah, it's definitely a direct reaction to all that new stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would disappear if the stress related to your freelance business were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would, yeah. But like I was saying a minute ago, the contract and all those changes with the business are still happening right now. So... I just haven't had a stretch of time where the stress or the fallout from it has been over for months to actually see if the anxiety completely disappears. It's just hard to say for sure until it's actually over.\n\nDoctor: Have you consistently felt anxious about your freelance business for more than six months?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been that long. It's really only been going on for about a month, right around the time I started trying to shift my art into an actual, formal freelance business.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's more of a constant, heavy feeling of stress that just builds up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or tasks because of feelings of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly just what I mentioned earlier. I definitely avoid the freelance stuff—like opening client emails or looking at the invoices. I just put them off because dealing with them makes me too anxious. But outside of that... no, I'm not really avoiding other situations or anything. It's really just those specific business tasks.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety mainly about your freelance business or does it also affect other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It's really just about the freelance business. I mean, like I said, it kind of spills over when I end up forgetting to pay my own personal bills because I'm so stressed out. But the actual anxiety itself... it's just tied to the business and dealing with the clients. The rest of my life is pretty normal, honestly. I don't really feel anxious about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My relationships are fine. I mean, I might be a little distracted sometimes if I'm stressing over an email I'm avoiding, but... no, I still see my friends and talk to my family just fine. It hasn't really caused any issues with them.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific happened related to your freelance business that started making you feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. I started getting this overwhelmed—and, you know, avoidant about everything—about a month after I took on this community arts contract. I was trying to make the business more formal at the time, and I guess that's when it all just started feeling like too much to handle.\n\nDoctor: Did the overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start immediately after taking on the community arts contract or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned just a second ago, there was a little bit of a delay. It was about a month after I took on that contract and started trying to make the business more formal. So it wasn't right away... it just took a few weeks for it all to really hit me.\n\nDoctor: Did your overwhelming feelings and avoidance of freelance tasks start after some significant change or stressor in your life, like taking on the community arts contract?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was just saying, that was definitely the main thing. I started getting really overwhelmed and avoidant about a month after I took on that community arts contract and tried to make the business more formal. That was the big change that set all this off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant emotional or behavioral symptoms, such as depression, in addition to the anxiety and avoidance related to your freelance business?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I've been feeling depressed or anything like that. It's really just the stress and the anxiety about the work, and wanting to avoid it. But I don't feel sad or down in the dumps.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance of freelance tasks would improve if the stressor, such as the community arts contract, were removed or resolved?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would, yeah. But, um, the contract and all those business changes are still happening right now. So I haven't really had a stretch of time where the stressor or its fallout has been over for months to see if the anxiety actually goes away. It's all just still ongoing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would go away if you were able to find a way to successfully manage the stress of your freelance business?\n\nPatient: I mean, I'm not really sure. I haven't figured out how to manage it yet, so it's hard to say if the anxiety would actually go away. I'd like to think it would, but I just don't know right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance of freelance tasks are a direct reaction to the stress of taking on the community arts contract and trying to formalize your business?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That's actually exactly why I came in. Ever since I tried to turn my art into a real freelance business, I just... I keep getting so wound up over invoices and client messages. And then I avoid them until I'm sitting there crying at my kitchen table, or I end up forgetting normal stuff like paying my own bills. So, yeah, it's definitely a direct reaction to all that new stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would disappear if the stress related to your freelance business were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would, yeah. But like I was saying a minute ago, the contract and all those changes with the business are still happening right now. So... I just haven't had a stretch of time where the stress or the fallout from it has been over for months to actually see if the anxiety completely disappears. It's just hard to say for sure until it's actually over.\n\nDoctor: Have you consistently felt anxious about your freelance business for more than six months?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been that long. It's really only been going on for about a month, right around the time I started trying to shift my art into an actual, formal freelance business.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would completely go away if you were able to cope with the stress of your freelance business or find a way to make it less overwhelming?\n\nPatient: I think it probably would go away, yeah. If I could actually handle the business side of things without getting so overwhelmed, I'd probably be fine. But... well, like I said, I'm still right in the middle of it. The contract and all the changes are happening right now. So I haven't really had a period where the stress or the mess it's causing has been over for months, you know? I haven't had the chance to see what I'd feel like if it was resolved.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing excessive worry about your freelance business for more days than not?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about it when I'm trying to work on it or when I get an email about a contract, but it's not like I'm sitting around worrying about it every single day, most of the time. It's more tied to when I actually have to deal with the business stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would completely go away if the stress related to your freelance business were removed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Like I was saying a minute ago, if you just took away all the business stress and the contracts entirely... I really don't think I'd be feeling like this. I'd probably just go back to normal. But I guess I won't know for absolute certain until it's actually gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety and avoidance would completely go away if the stress related to your freelance business were removed and you were able to go back to making art without the formal business aspects?\n\nPatient: I think it would, yes. But like I mentioned, the contract stuff and all these changes with the business are still going on right now. I haven't really had a period where the stress or the fallout from it has been over for months, so I can't say for sure how I'd feel after it's completely done. But yeah, if I could just go back to making art without the business side, I think I'd be fine.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
